10 月通胀点评

关注国际能源价格走势的不确定性   
10 月通胀走势和结构特点与 9 月基本一致；CPI 方面， 同比增速维持在1.0%
下方波动，主要受内需偏弱、服务价格季节性波动、能源和黄金价格输入性
因素影响；PPI 同比增速较 9 月继续上行，能源、化工、有色、计算机通信
等行业仍是主要支撑因素；10 月能源类价格是拉动通胀的重要分项，但考虑
到美国中期选举后，民主党掌握国会可能对中东局势产生重要影响，因此需
要关注能源价格走势对通胀的潜在影响。 
 10 月 CPI 环比持平，同比增长 0.7%，核心 CPI 同比增长 0.8%，服务价
格同比增长 0.7%，消费品价格同比增长 0.8%。 
 从分项同比看 ，10 月同比增长的是交通通信 （5.5%） 、 医疗保健 （2.4%）、
其他用品和服务（1.8%）、教育文化娱乐（ 1.5%）、衣着（1.1%）和生
活用品及服务（ 0.2%），同比增速下降的是居住（ -0.3%）和食品烟酒
（-1.6%），同比增速较 9 月上升的是交通通信和教育文化娱乐，同比增
速较 9 月下降的是衣着、医疗保健、食品烟酒和其他用品和服务。  
 短期内关注中东局势和国际能源价格。10 月影响 CPI 的因素较多，国内
方面，食品价格波动较大，受假期因素影响服务价格季节性上行，海外
方面，能源和黄金价格的输入性影响明显分化。从同比看， 10 月食品价
格影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.49 个百分点 ， 汽油价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63
个百分点，扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.30 个百分
点 ， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.34 个百分点 ； 从影响较大的分项看，
鲜菜 、 猪肉和鲜果合计影响CPI 同比下降约 0.54 个百分点 ， 鸡蛋影响CPI
同比上涨约 0.05 个百分点，牛肉和羊肉合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.09 个
百分点，数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机四项合计影响
CPI 同比上涨约 0.26 个百分点，黄金饰品影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.02 个百
分点。我们认为 CPI 分项中，食品、服务等内需相关的产品价格走势相
对稳定，需要关注的是容易受海外因素影响的分项，包括但不限于能源
价格、黄金价格、通信工具产品价格等等。短期内建议关注美国中期选
举后，中东局势是否会发生变化。我们目前暂时维持对年内 CPI 同比增
速在 1%下方平稳波动的观点。 
 10 月 PPI 环比增长 0.7%， 同比增长5.7%， 其中生产资料同比增长7.4%，
生活资料同比下降 0.1%。PPIRM 环比增长 1.1%，同比增长 7.9%。 
 关注能源价格对 PPI 的扰动。10 月 PPI 同比增长 5.7%，涨幅明显高于万
得一致预期 5.4%，结构特点与 9 月较为一致，从分项价格变动看，影响
较大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业价格，从行业看主要还是
输入性影响为主的能源化工、受 AI 需求带动的电气设备和计算机通信，
以及季节性需求上升的煤炭 、 燃气等 。 我们认为年底之前PPI 或维持较高
的同比增速，但来自能源价格影响的不确定性上升，建议密切关注美国
中期选举后中东局势可能出现的变化。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行的风险 ；欧美经济回落速度 偏快；国际局
势复杂化。

### Page 2

2026 年 11 月 9 日 10 月通胀点评 2

CPI 
CPI 环比增速连续两个月回落。10 月 CPI 环比持平 ， 增速较9 月下降 0.2 个百分点 。 从分项环比看，
10 月环比上行的包括交通通信（1.0%）、教育文化娱乐（0.3%）、医疗保健（0.1%），环比增速下
降的是生活用品及服务（-0.2%）、食品烟酒（-0.5%）、其他用品和服务（-1.1%），衣着和居住环
比增速持平；10 月分项中较 9 月环比增速上升的仅有教育文化娱乐，环比增速较 9 月下降的是生活
用品及服务、医疗保健、其他用品和服务、衣着、食品烟酒。 
9 月 CPI 同比增速低于万得一致预期。10 月 CPI 同比增长 0.7%，较 9 月下降 0.2 个百分点，核心
CPI 同比增长 0.8%，较 9 月下降 0.2 个百分点 ， 服务价格同比增长0.7%，较 9 月下降 0.1 个百分点，
消费品价格同比增长 0.8%，较 9 月下降 0.2 个百分点。从分项同比看，10 月同比增长的是交通通信
（5.5%）、医疗保健（2.4%）、其他用品和服务（1.8%）、教育文化娱乐（1.5%）、衣着（1.1%）
和生活用品及服务（0.2%） ，同比增速下降的是居住（-0.3%）和食品烟酒（-1.6%），同比增速较 9
月上升的是交通通信和教育文化娱乐，同比增速较 9 月下降的是衣着、医疗保健、食品烟酒和其他
用品和服务。 
10 月食品价格分化加大。从环比看 ，10 月由上月上涨 0.4%转为下降 1.0%， 影响CPI 环比下降约 0.17
个百分点；食品中，气候条件适宜，鸡蛋、鲜菜和鲜果价格分别环比下降 6.3%、5.8%和 0.6%，合
计影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.13 个百分点 ； 市场供应充足 ， 水产品和猪肉价格分别环比下降1.3%和1.0%，
合计影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.04 个百分点；牛肉和羊肉价格分别环比上涨 1.6%和 0.8%，涨幅均有回
落。从同比看，食品价格同比下降 2.9%，降幅比上月扩大 1.2 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.49
个百分点；食品中，鲜菜、猪肉和鲜果价格分别同比下降 16.9%、8.6%和 3.8%，合计影响 CPI 同比
下降约 0.54 个百分点；粮食、食用油和奶类价格同比降幅在 0.5%-1.2%之间，合计影响 CPI 同比下
降约 0.03 个百分点；鸡蛋价格同比上涨 12.3%，涨幅比上月回落 5.1 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨
约 0.05 个百分点；牛肉和羊肉价格分别同比上涨 8.7%和 5.8%，合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.09 个百
分点。 
非食品价格分化，其中既有输入性影响也有季节性影响 。从环比看，10 月服务价格由上月环比下降
0.2%转为环比上涨 0.1%，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点；服务中，国庆假期出行人数增加，
宾馆住宿、飞机票和旅行社收费分别环比上涨 6.5%、3.3%和 2.4%，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04
个百分点； 工业消费品价格环比上涨0.3%， 涨幅比上月回落0.4 个百分点， 影响CPI 环比上涨约 0.10
个百分点；工业消费品中，受国际市场价格变动影响，国内汽油价格环比上涨 4.2%，涨幅比上月回
落 0.7 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.14 个百分点；黄金饰品价格环比下降 5.9%，降幅比上月扩
大 3.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.03 个百分点。从同比看，10 月汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%，
涨幅比上月扩大 5.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63 个百分点，带动能源价格同比涨幅由上月
的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%；扣除能源的工业消费品价格同比上涨 1.2%，涨幅比上月回落 0.4 个百
分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.30 个百分点，其中，数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机
价格分别同比上涨 45.4%、29.3%、23.0%和 16.7%，四项合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.26 个百分点；
黄金饰品价格同比上涨 4.5%， 涨幅比上月回落18.0 个百分点， 影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.02 个百分点；
服务价格同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅比上月回落 0.1 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.34 个百分点。 
短期内关注中东局势和国际能源价格。10 月影响 CPI 的因素较多，国内方面，食品价格波动较大，
受假期因素影响服务价格季节性上行，海外方面，能源和黄金价格的输入性影响明显分化。 从同比
看，10 月食品价格影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.49 个百分点，汽油价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63 个百分
点， 扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.30 个百分点， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约
0.34 个百分点；从影响较大的分项看，鲜菜、猪肉和鲜果合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.54 个百分点，
鸡蛋影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.05 个百分点， 牛肉和羊肉合计影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.09 个百分点， 数据
存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机四项合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.26 个百分点，黄金饰品
影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.02 个百分点。我们认为 CPI 分项中，食品、服务等内需相关的产品价格走势
相对稳定，需要关注的是容易受海外因素影响的分项，包括但不限于能源价格、黄金价格、通信工
具产品价格等等。短期内建议关注美国中期选举后，中东局势是否会发生变化。我们目前暂时维持
对年内 CPI 同比增速在 1%下方平稳波动的观点。

PPI 
10 月 PPI 同比增速超预期。10 月 PPI 环比增长 0.7%，较 9 月下降 0.5 个百分点，其中生产资料环
比增长 0.9%，生活资料环比下降 0.1%。10 月 PPI 同比增长 5.7%，超出万得一致预期，较 9 月上升
0.7 个百分点，其中生产资料同比增长 7.4%，较 9 月上升 0.9 个百分点，生活资料同比下降 0.1%，
较 9 月持平。

### Page 4

2026 年 11 月 9 日 10 月通胀点评 4

10 月 PPIRM 环比增长 1.1%，同比增长 7.9%，较 9 月上升 1.0 个百分点；从构成来看，10 月同比增
速上升较多的是燃料、动力类（ 18.6%），化工原料类（ 16.8%）和有色金属材料类（ 16.7%），同
比增速下降的是建筑材料类（-1.9%），黑色金属材料类（-1.1%）和木材及纸浆类（-0.5%）；所有
品类中，同比增速较 9 月上升较多的是燃料、动力类和化工原料类，同比增速下降的是有色金属材
料类，黑色金属材料类、木材及纸浆类和其他工业原料 。 
10 月主要行业中出厂价格同比增速较高的是，煤炭开采和洗选业（ 36.6%），石油和天然气开采业
（35.0%） 和石油 、 煤炭及其他燃料加工业 （29.9%） ， 同比增速较低的是黑色金属矿采选业 （-6.4%），
酒、饮料和精制茶制造业（-5.2%）和电力、热力的生产和供应业（-3.8%）；同比增速较 9 月上升
较多的是石油和天然气开采业，石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工 业和化学原料及化学制品制造业，下降
的仅有有色金属矿采选业，黑色金属矿采选业、有色金属冶炼及压延加工业和造纸及纸制品业 。 
输入性因素和季节性因素影响较大 。10 月 PPI 环比运行的主要特点：一是输入性和季节性因素影响
主要能源相关行业价格上涨。石油和天然气开采业、煤炭开采和洗选业价格、精炼石油产品制造、
有机化学原料制造 、 煤炭加工 、 燃气生产和供应业6 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.61 个百分点，
超过总涨幅的八成。二是产能治理有序推进、人工智能快速发展带动部分原材料及装备制造行业价
格上行。三是消费品市场供给充足、需求 平稳，价格总体稳定。从同比看，价格上涨的行业中，煤
炭开采和洗选业 、 石油和天然气开采业 、 石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业 、 化学原料和化学制品制造业、
燃气生产和供应业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业涨幅比上月均扩大，有色金属矿采选业、有
色金属冶炼和压延加工业涨幅比上月均回落，电气机械和器材制造业涨幅与上月相同，上述 9 个行
业合计影响 PPI 同比上涨约 6.14 个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 0.64 个百分点。价格下拉影响最大
的 5 个行业为：酒饮料和精制茶制造业、医药制造业、非金属矿物制品业、汽车制造业、电力热力
生产和供应业，降幅比上月均有收窄，合计影响 PPI 同比下降约 0.60 个百分点，下拉影响比上月减
少 0.06 个百分点。 
关注能源价格对 PPI 的扰动。10 月 PPI 同比增长 5.7%，涨幅明显高于万得一致预期 5.4%，结构特
点与 9 月较为一致，从分项价格变动看，影响较大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业价格，
从行业看主要还是输入性影响为主的能源化工 、受 AI 需求带动的电气设备和计算机通信 ，以及季节
性需求上升的煤炭、燃气等。我们认为年底之前 PPI 或维持较高的同比增速，但来自能源价格影响
的不确定性上升，建议密切关注美国中期选举后中东局势可能出现的变化。

本报告准确表述了证券分析师的个人观点。该证券分析师声明，本人未在公司内、外部机构兼任有损本人独立性与客
观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。  
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报
告有关情况 。 如有投资者于未经授权的公众媒体看到或从其他机构获得本研究报告的 ， 请慎重使用所获得的研究报告，
以防止被误导，中银国际证券股份有限公司不对其报告理解和使用承担任何责任。  
评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 隨著法國距離下屆總統大選僅剩六個月，極左翼與政府間的政治對立升溫，然而
法國政府急需推動預算案以控制財政赤字與龐大債務。在內政危機加劇與工會
預告 11月大罷工的背景下，市場對法國債務可持續性的擔憂升溫，將帶動法德
公債利差維持高檔，進而推升歐元區主權風險溢酬並拖累歐元表現。
 上週路透最新調查顯示，在 100 位受訪經濟學家中，高達 94%預期聯準會將於
10 月會議維持利率在 3.75%-4.00% 不變，惟全員一致預估 12 月將重啟升息。
儘管美國受 AI 投資熱潮帶動、經濟成長強勁，且美伊戰事推升油價突破百元
大關、加徵關稅使 PCE 通膨至少在 2028 年前難以回到 2% 目標，官員仍傾
向審慎行事。不過逾四成專家警告，聯準會恐落後通膨形勢，後續面臨至少再升
息兩次甚至擴大升息幅度的上行風險，預期美元大幅回檔的空間小，逢低承接買
盤依然強勁。
 路透最新調查顯示，受日圓疲軟與中東戰事推升能源及進口成本、加劇通膨壓力

影響，近七成受訪經濟學家預計日本央行將於 12月再度升息至 1.50%，延續 6
月與 9 月以來的較快緊縮步伐；同時，逾半數專家預估終端利率將上看 2.00%
或更高。不過，高達近四分之三受訪者警告，若高市早苗內閣提名的鴿派委員再
次投下反對票，恐壓抑日圓表現。在歐美央行年底前亦可能升息的背景下，日本
央行維持較快的緊縮節奏，有助於防止美日、歐日利差進一步擴大，避免日圓在
年底遭遇新一輪劇烈拋售，為日圓提供實質的下檔保護。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.360，低點在 32.704，以 32.508作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣走貶，周初，外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢，台幣跳空開低，跌破今
年 7 月的低點 32.528；隨著台股表現不佳，外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣續創低位，
最低來到 32.704周內低點；而後台股低檔反彈，外資由匯出轉為雙向進出，加以出
口商積極進場拋售美金，台幣由貶轉升至 32.500下方；臨近周末，市場擔憂颱風來
襲周五可能休市，增添連續假期變數，外資再度大幅賣超台股並匯出，令台幣又跌
回 32.600上方，終場接在央行調節之下收在 32.508。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。近期全球科技股因 AI 類股估值偏高及融資風險疑慮
而持續修正，市場風險偏好降溫，股市面臨修正，外資連續賣超台股並匯出，台幣
走勢持續面臨壓力。另一方面，美伊戰事和談進展的消息反覆，令國際油價節節攀
升，突破每桶 110美元上方，引發通膨升溫的擔憂，支撐利率維持在高檔的預期，
美元指數也在 101.5 上方高位震盪，將不利台幣的表現。盤勢上來看，外資隨著股
市回檔修正表現偏向賣超台股並匯出，台幣偏弱，不過本周適逢月底，預計匯價逢
高仍持續有出口商拋售美金的需求，將限制台幣走貶的幅度。綜合以上，預估本週
台幣走勢偏弱。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣走升。周初中國公布9月工業生產物價指數 5.5%，表現優於預期的
5.4%(前值 5.2%)，且第三季 GDP表現 4.5%，也優於預期的 4.4%(前值 4.3%)，基本
面穩健支撐人民幣升破 6.6800 整數關口；不過周間美元指數持續於 101.5 上方高位
震盪，加以美伊戰事未見緩解，國際油價持續飆升，突破每桶 110 美元上方，人民
幣於 6.6800下方高檔震盪；臨近周末，人行中間價格連續走升，升破6.7200整數關
口，週五最高來到 6.7180，顯示中國人行允許人民幣溫和升值的走勢，終場境外人
民幣升破 6.6700整數關口 ， 收在6.6690， 週線升值160點 ， 境內人民幣則收在6.6704，
週線升值 166 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價持續偏升，將持續帶
動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現；就國際市場來看，最
新利率期貨顯示 ， 本周聯準會利率決議有9成的機率維持利率在 3.75%~4.00%不變 ，
不過年底前仍有一碼的升息空間，加以地緣政治風險未見緩解，能源價格高檔震盪
推升通膨與利率預期，美元指數維持在 101.5 上方高檔震盪，將限制人民幣升值的
速度跟幅度；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價
維持在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預
估本週人民幣偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6300-6.6900

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌。週初，USD/JPY 大致在 158 的整數關卡上下波動；
週間，AI相關概念股遭拋售，加之中東局勢緊張推升避險需求，10年期美債殖利率
跌破 5%，避險資金亦流入日圓，帶動 USD/JPY 下行最低來至 156.67；臨近週末，
市場避險需求持續加之日本 9 月通膨數據高於日本央行 2%目標，USD/JPY 一度下
跌至 156.06，後逢低買盤進場跌幅有所收斂，終場收在 156.86，週線下跌 113點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下： 一、美國10 月 S&P製造業 PMI
為 56.0高於 9 月的 55.9且為 53個月高點，服務業則為 57.9低於 9月的 58.8，綜合
PMI為 57.6亦低於 9 月的 58.4，顯示美國商業活動雖略有放緩但仍處於擴張，經濟
基本面依然穩健將持續支撐美元；此外，中東局勢惡化使能源供應風險上升，布蘭
特原油價格突破 116美元，為六個月以來高點，也將有利USD/JPY上行。二、日本
9 月全國整體消費者物價指數（CPI）年增 2.1%，且 9 月核心 CPI年增 2.1%，為九
個月以來首次超過日本央行 2%的目標，強化市場對日本央行短期升息的預期，預計
為日圓帶來支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY匯價處於各天期均線之
下且接連跌破 158 及 157 關卡，後在 156 整數關卡暫時獲得支撐，顯示逢低仍有買
盤進場，將限縮下行幅度。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較
高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.00-158.50

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD震盪下跌。週初，EUR/USD在 1.1200整數關卡窄幅波動；週間
受到油價及美債殖利率回落，外加法國社會局勢略見緩解影響，帶動匯價反彈至
1.1260遇到賣壓後回落，後續大致在1.1190至 1.1250間波動；臨近週末，歐元區10
月份 PMI表現低迷，且市場對於法國政局和財政赤字擔憂持續，拖累 EUR/USD轉
若，匯價一度下行來至 1.1183，終場收在 1.1202，週線下跌 37點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD震盪偏空，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元區10月 PMI
顯示德、法表現分化，法國製造業及服務業均表現疲弱低於50榮枯線，拖累歐元區
10 月綜合 PMI 由 9 月 53.1 下滑至 51.9，為四個月以來新低，經濟基本面承壓將不
利 EUR/USD上行；此外，法國社會及政經局勢不穩導致法債殖利率突破5%，市場
對其財政及政治危機擔憂持續，也將不利歐元表現。二、美國上週初請失業金人數
降至 19.4萬人，為三個月以來新低，顯示勞動力市場依然穩健，且10月 S&P製造
業 PMI為 56.0續創 53個月新高，服務業及綜合PMI維持穩健，顯示美國經濟仍具
韌性，預期將限縮 EUR/USD上行空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
各天期均線維持空頭排列，且匯價持續承壓於月線之下，顯示短線空方趨勢仍未改
變，在能有效突破上方月線前仍以震盪偏空看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週
呈現震盪偏空的可能性較高。

新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.19-115.10.22  
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.825%  
30天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68%  
  公債－10年期- A15109 2.000%~2.002% 
      － 5 年期- A15108     1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，儘管市場預期強烈颱風「薔琵」來襲，週五金融市場可能
因颱風假而暫停交易，銀行間多預留帳上資金以應不時之需，但市況仍屬充
裕，即便有新增調度需求，也很容易滿足，令短率朝區間低位移動。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.825%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.68%，利率低位較上週下跌約 1 個基本點。

二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 週四於等殖系統成交在 2.002%，
5 年期指標券 A15108則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.88%。 
週二 10 年期公債標售得標利率 2.001%，落於市場預期區間下緣，並創 2008
年以來新高，顯示市場持續反映經濟成長與通膨預期。 
考量通膨前景及國際債市走勢仍具不確定性，市場觀望氣氛濃厚；惟第四季公
債供給相對有限，可望抑制殖利率大幅彈升。預期短線台債殖利率維持區間整
理，5 年券及 10 年券交易區間分別估於 1.82%~1.84%及 1.98%~2.01%。

NYPCB
Valuation
Our DCF-based TP for NYPCB is NT$1,700. With a risk-free rate of 5.00% (10-year government bond rate), a market risk
premium of 7% and an equity beta of 1.22 (YTD weekly share price), we calculate NYPCB's WACC as 13.4%. Our TP is equivalent
to 30x/13x 2027E EPS/BVPS, which reflects its high P/E multiple during upcycles. NYPCB traded at 15-20x P/E during its
2003-2008 upcycle driven by PC/NB demand strength. In 2010-11 when the market was recovering from the global financial

crisis, NYPCB traded at 25-35x P/E. In the last 2019-22 upcycle when ABF was in shortage, NYPCB traded at 30-40x P/E.
Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the NYPCB shares from reaching our target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected ABF
demand from key customers; 2) BT business seasonality weakness and fewer-than-expected price hikes; and 3) severe T glass
shortages that cause a bottleneck to its production.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

3Q26 results overview 
AWSC’s 3Q26 core business OP was NT$209mn, which was lower 
than both GSe/BBG consensus of NT$226mn/251mn, mainly due to 
the ongoing impact from rising material cost, leading to a 24.3% GM 
(lower than GSe/BBG consensus GM of 25.6%/29.3%), despite UTR 
in 3Q26 remaining stable at 65-70%. 
For earnings, AWSC 3Q26 EPS was 3% higher than GSe but was 9% 
lower than BBG consensus on the contracted GM. 
Valuation 
We maintain our Neutral rating with a 12M TP of NT$147, based on 
27x 4Q26-3Q27E P/E, which is line with the company’s past 10-year 
forward P/E multiple, and in line with the inventory build-up period 
average P/E.

### Page 3

Investment Thesis and TP & risks

AWSC is one of the largest GaAS foundries globally, with 10-15% market share in the 
GaAS foundry industry in 2025. We expect the company to enjoy solid share gain 
opportunity in the long term, considering its much lower than peers’ production cost 
and better production efficiency. However, given the high inventory level in the supply 
chain, and the slower-than-expected Android smartphone PA demand in coming 
quarters, we expect relatively slow profit growth in the near term. 
We are Neutral rated given the company’s relatively slow profit growth in coming 
quarters. We think the stock is priced fairly, trading at its historical upcycle average P/E 
multiple, despite the long-term market share growth opportunity. We forecast AWSC’s 
net income to grow at a 34% 2026-28E CAGR. The key up/downside risks are (1) 
easing/rising competition in the China PA foundry market, (2) 
faster/slower-than-expected progress on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade trends, and (3) 
faster/slower-than expected progress with new businesses, including LEO satellite, 
drones and datacom.  
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$147 is based on a target 27x 
4Q26-3Q27 P/E, which is in line with the company’s past 10-year forward P/E multiple 
and the inventory build-up period average P/E. We consider this multiple appropriate 
given the soft demand outlook and our positive view on the company’s longer-term 
share gain opportunity. 
The key up/downside risks are 1) easing/rising competition in the China PA foundry 
market, (2) faster/slower-than-expected progress on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade trends, and (3) 
faster/slower-than expected progress with new businesses, including LEO satellite, 
drones and datacom.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

주가 추이

3Q 큰 폭의 판매 감소, 매출/영업이익 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영 업 이 익 은 각각 44.6조원(-4.5% 이하 Y oY)과 2.4조원
(-5.4%) 기록하며 시장 기대치 하회 전망. 3Q 매출액은 글로벌 판매 부진 지속
과 환율 하락, 그리고 친환경차 판매 성장 둔화 등의 영향에 기인. 특히, 3Q 판
매량은 918.7만대(-11.5% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동기 대비 큰 폭 감소하며 매출
액에 부정적 영향. 이는 7월과 8월 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생
산 물량 차질이 발 생 한 부분에 기인. 파업은 기존 물량/믹스 개선에 기 여 했 던 
글로벌 HEV 판매도 둔화(+3.4%)시키면서 매출에 부정적 영향을 심화. 
3Q 수 익 성 은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입에 따른 원가 상승 영향과 친환경차 
판매 감소에 따른 부정적 믹스 영향 불 가 피. 이에 시장 기대치인 3.0조원을 크
게 하회할 전망. 다만, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지면서 판매보증비 
외화환산손익 영향은 다소 개선(0.4%p) 예상되며, 관세 영향 또한 전년 동기 
대비 약 1조원(2.1%p) 감소하면서 수익성 악화 영향을 일부 만 회 할 전망.  
’27년 지연된 신차효과 가시화로 반등 여력 충분 
4Q부터는 3Q 파업 영향 등으로 지연된 신차 확대 본격화되고, 현지 수요 높은 
차종 중심의 물량 확대를 통해 매출 회복 및 이익 성장 가시화가 기대. 아 반 떼
와 투싼 FMC 확대로 국내 및 해외 판매 둔화가 점진적 만회되고, 지난 9월부
터 터키에서 아이오닉3 생산이 본격화되면서 유럽에서의 시장 부진 회복이 기
대. 또한, GV80과 G80 HEV 모델 출시로 내년부터 국내/북미 중심 판매 확대
되며 올해 부진했던 물량/믹스 개선에 기여할 것으로 기대. ’27년 HMGMA에
서 HEV 신차 생산 투입 예정되어 있어 상 대 적 으 로 높은 수요가 지속되는 북미
에서의 HEV 현지 생산 확대로 가동률 증 가 와 관세 부담 완화도 수익성 개선에 
기여할 것으로 기대. 
부정적 환율 고려 필요하나 ’27년 강한 증익 기대  
’27년 동사 영 업 이 익 추 정 치 는 12.4조원(+22.3%)으로 ’26년 감 익 에 서 ’27년 
증익 전환 예상. 최근 환율 추세 고려 시 내년 상 반 기 까 지 부정적인 환율 영향 
확대 가 능 성 존재하나, 기존 노후 차 종 에 서 현지 수요 대응 가능한 신차 중심 
생산/판매 확대로 물량/믹스 개선되며 부 정 적 환율 영향 상쇄 기대.  
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-11.8%)과 상반기 적 용 한 코 스 피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)에 따라 목 표 주 가 를 670,000원으로 하향. 그러나, 연 내 RMAC 운영 
성 과 와 RA 설립, 12 월 모셔널 자 율 주 행 상용화와 웨 이 모向 로 보 택 시 
양산/공급 등 미래 사 업 에 서 의 가 시 성 도 여전히 기대 가능한 부분.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 8 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 308: Brent at $77_bbl_ 3 calls for energy markets

Given the level of uncertainty in energy markets this year and
with little sign of a change going into next year, any call on
energy markets for 2027 is a low-conviction call. But here are
our 3 calls for energy markets for 2027
Warren Patterson
Oil ﬂows are recovering, but uncertainty remains
How to read our three calls for 2027
Source: ING
ING's base call: Brent to average
$77/bbl next year

### Page 2

Oil markets have been on a roller coaster this year given
events in the Middle East. However, we are going into year-
end with oil ﬂows from the region near pre-war levels despite
the lack of a deal between the US and Iran. While oil ﬂows are
normalising, the way oil is coming out of the region is
anything but normal, which is reﬂected in the surge in tanker
shipping rates, given the very ineﬃcient oil ﬂows.
Our base case assumes occasional ﬂare-ups between the US

and Iran, meaning a full normalisation of Persian Gulf
supplies remains some way oﬀ and the oil market is likely to
retain a degree of risk premium. However, provided there are
no signiﬁcant or prolonged disruptions, we expect the market
surplus to build over the course of 2027, leaving Brent to
average around $77/bbl. A deal between the US and Iran
would add further downside risk to this view.
Our risky call: OPEC will see more member
exits
We believe harmony within OPEC could be tested, with the

possibility of further members leaving the group. This follows the
UAE’s departure earlier this year. Post-Persian Gulf disruptions, any
breakdown or weakening of the group risks adding more supply to
the market, which would potentially leave further downside to our
base case price forecasts for Brent in 2027.
The most likely candidate to leave OPEC is Venezuela, with the US’s
growing inﬂuence over the Venezuelan oil industry following the
capture and arrest of President Maduro at the start of 2026. But we

believe that some Persian Gulf producers may also question their
membership, particularly if disagreements emerge over the
capacity numbers used by the group to set production targets. Iraq
has already made noise around production targets, and following
the exit of the UAE, some members may be looking at them
enviously, with the UAE pumping record volumes of crude since
leaving the bloc.

Our bold call: TTF to trade sub-€40/MWh
before the end of next year
European gas prices have strengthened signiﬁcantly in 2026. But

what goes up must come down, and we believe prices could fall
sharply next year. There are clearly plenty of assumptions here,
most importantly a normalisation of Persian Gulf LNG ﬂows in
early 2027. If that happens, we believe TTF could trade below
€40/MWh by end-2027.
On the demand side, higher prices are already driving some
industrial demand destruction, while we are deep in the gas-to-coal
switching range. Furthermore, El Niño could bring milder weather to

parts of Europe and East Asia. While far from guaranteed, this could
reduce heating demand in winter. So, we potentially come out of
the 2026/27 winter with the EU gas balance not as tight as many
fear, putting downward pressure on prices.
We also have signiﬁcant US LNG export capacity coming onto the
market, which should help with storage ahead of the 2027/28
winter. If the situation in the Persian Gulf normalises, we also have
new capacity starting up in Qatar.

## Document 318: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book 1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.
Investment Implications
Oil market conditions remain a swing
factor for the outlook. A shift lower in
oil risk premia and improvement on
the refining front would go a long way

toward easing inflation and interest
rates while bolstering risk-asset
appetite. A gradual increase in Fixed
Income portfolio duration continues to
make sense, in our view.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings hav e generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than tw o-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.” 2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market. 2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of inv estment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractiv e in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market mo ves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting go vernment
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff

* “Pop the Champagne,” Portfolio Insights, Chief Investment Office, August 2026.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

## Document 340: EcoNews of 2 November 2026 (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 2 November 2026 (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-02

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing

5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. Data releases confirmed resilient to strong growth and
above-forecast inflation in most advanced economies, with France lag-
ging behind and earning a rating outlook downgrade from Fitch as a
result. Fed, ECB and BoJ remained on hold, as expected, and on track
for December hikes. Oil continues to fluctuate between escalation fears

and hopes for a return to the negotiating table. The tech sell-off in
equity markets intensified despite strong earnings, as markets balk at
large capex plans. This helped bond yields ease from recent highs.
EUROZONE
Resilience confirmed despite tightening credit. GDP expanded by a
stronger-than-expected 0.4% q/q in Q3 (0.2% ex-Ireland), with Spain,
Germany and the Netherlands contributing to the bulk of non-Irish
growth. We increase our growth forecast to 1.6% in 2027 (+0.1pp),

driven by further improving sentiment in manufacturing. Bank lending
conditions tightened further in Q3 for businesses, especially SMEs,
driven by France and Germany, while corporate and housing-loan de -
mand weakened. The ECB kept its deposit rate at 2.5% and signaled it
was firmly on track for a (likely final) December hike. President Lagarde
also confirmed she would step down early, on February 7, setting off
the succession race. EU leaders are expected to decide jointly on the

three Board members departing between January and May (President,
chief economist, and market operations), likely at their mid-December
meeting. Official candidates to succeed Lagarde are BIS Head Pablo
Hernandez de Cos and former Dutch central bank Governor Klaas Knot.
To watch next week: October flash CPI (Wednesday).
France: Another growth setback, with silver linings
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 as a sharp drop in aeronauti -
cal production hit exports and investment. However, services out -

put grew by a robust 0.3% q/q, and household consumption also
grew more strongly than forecasted (+0.4%). While 2026 will ine -
vitably be knocked down (to 0.3%), we forecast 0.8% for 2027.
Nevertheless, Fitch issued an unscheduled rating outlook downgrade
to negative territory, on grounds that fiscal, growth and ad -
justment prospects have deteriorated since its August review.
A new review could be concluded as early as February. Harmonised
inflation accelerated to 3.8% y/y in October, with both energy and

non-energy contributing. Meanwhile, producer price inflation accele -
rated to 8.2% y/y in September. Insee’s index of household confidence
fell to 80, matching its 2013 all-time low, driven by a decline in ex -
pectations of its own financial situation. Private payroll employment
declined by 26,000 over Q3 (-0.1%). However, in previous quarters, the
decline in payroll employment was more than offset by the increase in
self-employment (not released until November 27).

To watch next week: September industrial production (Thursday); Sep-
tember foreign trade (Friday).
Germany: Growth holds up as inflation slows
GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3, with consumption rising slightly but in -
vestment declining and export growth slowing. HICP inflation fell from
3.3% to 2.8% in October following energy-tax cuts and fuel discounts,
but core inflation also slowed, to 2.1%. Business confidence (Ifo survey)
reached its highest level in more than three years, led by manufactu -

ring, which is back in positive territory for the first time since 2023.
By contrast, consumer confidence deteriorated to its lowest since May,
due to growing concerns about inflation, which are weighing on consu-
mers’ spending intentions.
To watch next week: September retail sales (Monday); September fac-
tory orders (Thursday); September industrial production (Friday).
Italy: Stagnant growth meets energy-driven inflation surge
GDP was flat in Q3, missing expectations, as positive domestic demand

was offset by net exports. Economic sentiment improved, but HICP in -
flation jumped from 4.2% to 5.6% in October, its highest level since
September 2023, mainly because of regulated energy prices; core in -
flation also rose, to a more moderate 2%. More positively, economic
sentiment rose to 100.2 in October 2026 (from 99.4 in September),
despite the Eurozone decline. The improvement was driven by services
and construction. The Italian cabinet approved a budget focused on

middle-class tax cuts, financed partly by levies on banks, energy and
gambling companies. x
UNITED KINGDOM
A cautious budget buys breathing space. The budget offered 98 small
cost-of-living alleviation measures and £4.1 billion in household ener-
gy cost relief, while only modestly increasing taxes, including a tem -
porary rise in the bank surcharge. Its short-term stance is slightly ex -
pansionary, while major structural decisions on defence, pensions and

welfare have been postponed. The official budget watchdog, OBR, cut
its 2027 growth forecast to 1%, and firms’ inflation expectations conti-
nued to rise. UK gilts outperformed and equities advanced after the
budget. GBP rose to a six-week high against the dollar and a 19-month
high versus the euro as markets price a near-certain Bank of England
rate hike (to 4%) at this week meeting. We agree but, unlike market
pricing, we do not expect it to hike further.

To watch next week: Bank of England interest-rate decision (Thursday).
UNITED STATES
Fed awaiting more information despite strong growth. The Federal Re-
serve held its target range at 3.75–4.0%, with two members voting for a
25 bp increase, as Chair Warsh highlighted stronger growth, favourable
employment conditions and inflation that remains too high. Q3 GDP
accelerated to 3.4% annualised, powered by resilient consumption and
a 12.7% jump in business investment. Despite jobless claims remaining

near multi-decade lows, compensation costs adjusted for inflation de-
clined 0.4%, marking the second consecutive quarter in which labour
cost growth trailed price increases.
To watch next week: Midterm elections (Tuesday); October ISM ma -
nufacturing (Monday) and non-manufacturing (Wednesday); Q3 non-
farm productivity (Thursday); October non-farm payrolls (Friday).
JAPAN
BoJ on hold with underlying inflation “consistent with the 2% tar -
get”. The Bank of Japan kept its policy rate at 1.25%, with one dissent

in favour of a hike. Its outlook judged underlying inflation to be
consistent with the 2% target, a major shift in its assessment, although
Governor Ueda mentioned risks on both sides in his remarks, stres -
sing in particular the risk of inflation overshooting the target. It signals
continued gradual monetary adjustment, with a next hike expected in
December. Alongside the rate hold, the BOJ announced it would near -
ly triple the pace of ETF sales starting in January, to approximately

JPY 860 billion per year. Japanese equities shrugged off the announ -
cement.
To watch next week: Minutes of the September Bank of Japan meeting
(Thursday); September wage growth (Friday).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
EMERGING ECONOMIES
ASIA
China: Monetary policy reform and no growth signals
The PBOC disclosed a 1.25% overnight reverse-repo rate and an -
nounced that the overnight rate would become its principal policy
lever, starting 2nd November. It also pledged to develop cross-border
renminbi payments and introduced a liquidity backstop for non-bank

financial institutions. The 5th Plenum of the 20 th Central Committee of
the CCP focused on party discipline and crackdown on corrupt cadres,
without broader policy signals. Chinese equities had their worst month
since 2016, falling 8.5% in October, as doubts over AI earnings deepe -
ned investor pessimism. The PMIs published on 30 th October suggest
economic activity is losing momentum; in the manufacturing sector,
the RatingDog PMI fell slightly to 52 (from 52.1) while the official PMI

fell more sharply and dropped below 50 (49.3 vs. 50.1 in the previous
month).
South Korea and Taiwan: AI boom lifts growth even higher
In Korea, GDP grew by 1.0% q/q and 3.4% y/y in Q3, supported by boo -
ming semiconductor exports linked to global AI demand. The expansion
was uneven, however, as private-consumption growth slowed marke -
dly. In Taiwan, GDP surged by 3.2% q/q and 14.4% y/y in Q3, beating
expectations and accelerating from the previous quarter as technology

firms expanded capacity to meet strong demand.
EMEA
Poland: Lower inflation gives monetary policy some room
Headline inflation declined from 4.0% to 3.5% in October as electricity,
gas and transport fuel prices rose more slowly, helped by a temporary
fuel-price cap. Rates are likely to remain unchanged in the near term,
although renewed zloty weakness means a hike cannot be ruled out.
Saudi Arabia: Reserves fall as regional pressures intensify
Official reserve assets declined by 4.4% m/m in September to

SAR 1.75 tn, their lowest level since December, with foreign-currency
assets recording particularly sharp monthly and annual declines.
Türkiye: Pressure on lira and FX reserves
The lira headed for its largest monthly fall in 15 months and cen -
tral-bank reserves came under renewed pressure. This is the conse -
quence of the warning by MSCI that Türkiye could lose its emer -
ging-market status if measures to improve transparency and market
supervision following the Fund scandal are deemed insufficient.

LATIN AMERICA
Brazil’s post-election rout: stocks and real plunge, yields surge. Bra -
zilian markets, expecting a Flavio Bolsonaro victory, sold off sharply
after Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva won a fourth presidential term. The Ibo-
vespa fell 14% Monday — its largest daily drop since COVID-19 — and
ended the week down 18%. The real fell over 11% intraday and ended
the week down 8% against the euro. Meanwhile the yield on Brazil’s
10-year government bonds rose 250 bp to 15.5%, reaching its highest

level in over a decade. The selloff reflects fiscal concerns over pledges
to raise the minimum wage and Bolsa familia benefits and to offer free
public transport on weekends and bank holidays. Finance Minister Da-
rio Durigan’s pledge to seek a higher 2027 primary surplus, without a
new target, did little to reassure markets.
COMMODITIES
The oil market continues to fluctuate between escalation fears and
hopes for a return to the negotiating table. Brent prices were down by

5% over the week, reaching USD 109/b on Monday. Tensions on physical
supply continue to decline. Ditto for diesel crack spreads. On the back
of geopolitical tensions, lower-than-requested EU stock level and spe-
culation over a cold snap in Europe in November, European gas prices
(TTF) recorded another strong weekly rise and have breached the
EUR 100/MWh for the first time since Dec 2022). The Asian gas bench-
mark (JKM) is following the same trend but at a slower pace (4.6% w/w

to USD 32 /MBtu, equivalent to around EUR 96.3 /MWh).
TO WATCH NEXT WEEK
Global: Manufacturing PMIs for EM and final DM prints (Monday); final
services and composite PMIs (Wednesday).
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels
MBtu = Million British Thermal Units

Even in the period when France has run 50-plus years of consecutive deficits, its average
inflation has been broadly around US levels (c.4%) and lower than the likes of the UK, Italy and
Spain.

## Document 400: Our latest views on the major central banks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Our latest views on the major central banks.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Here’s our take on what could be next for the Federal Reserve,
the European Central Bank, the Bank of England, the Bank of
Japan and the People's Bank of China over the coming months

James Knightley, James Smith, Carsten Brzeski, Chris Turner and
Lynn Song
Federal Reserve
Strong growth, low unemployment and above-target inﬂation
prompted the Fed to hike rates in September and signal that at
least one additional rate increase is likely.
This was all the ammunition markets needed to price in at least
three additional 25bp moves. However, caution is creeping back
into market pricing with oﬃcials skipping the October Federal Open
Market Committee (FOMC) meeting despite higher-than-expected
inﬂation prints. Instead, the near-uniform message is that the Fed
is under no immediate pressure to hike amid growing evidence that
interest-rate-sensitive parts of the economy are cooling while wage
pressures look increasingly benign.
Cookie settings

### Page 2

Additional Fed rate hikes will do nothing to ease tensions in the
Middle East and likely do little to cool the strong pipeline of tech
investment. We think the Fed will hike once more, at the December
FOMC meeting, after which we expect a prolonged pause well into
2027.
European Central Bank
Another rate hike in December looks like a done deal, but looking
beyond this year, the outlook for the ECB is clearly less certain. In
fact, two main factors will be crucial for the ECB in 2027. One, the

intensity and duration of the war in the Middle East and, with it, the
trajectory of energy prices. Two, the degree to which higher energy
prices translate into broader inﬂationary pressures. While the ECB
expects these knock-on eﬀects to materialise in 2027, so far, actual
underlying inﬂation has remained well-behaved.
In our base case scenario, we expect energy prices to fall in 2027,
while weak ﬁnal demand limits companies' pricing power. This will

keep the current inﬂation wave mainly an energy price shock.
Against this background, the ECB can keep its powder dry in 2027
and might even consider a rate cut towards the end of the year.
Bank of England
We expect one more rate hike in February, after the BoE hiked rates
for the ﬁrst time since 2023 in early November. Yet we don't rule
out a hike sooner in December, if the energy crisis worsens. Inﬂation
is headed above 4% and the Bank is worried that the crisis has now

reached suﬃcient depth and longevity to justify further action.
Still, there's little evidence so far of second-round eﬀects. Food
inﬂation is remarkably benign. Our gauge of "energy intensive"
inﬂation is actually down this year. And the jobs market is
suﬃciently cool, meaning wage pressures are unlikely to build
materially. For now, it's too early for the Bank to let its guard down.
The data through winter and early spring will be crucial, particularly

the April CPI report released in May, which is heavily aﬀected by
annual price resets. If by then there are still minimal signs of
second-round eﬀects, we think the Bank will feel comfortable

### Page 3

turning more dovish by the summer. Ultimately, this could enable
the BoE to cut rates before the end of 2027.
Bank of Japan
BoJ policy normalisation will continue. In October, the BoJ reached
a milestone: ﬁnally hitting its 2% inﬂation target. That target had
been set in 2013. The BoJ openly admits that real interest rates are
negative. The debate naturally moves on to how quickly the 1.25%
policy rate will be taken to neutral – commonly seen in the 1.75-

2.00% area. We now look for 25bp hikes at the December and
March meetings as the quarterly tightening cycle continues.
Market expectations are a little more aggressive than our view,
looking for a 2.00% policy rate next summer. Our take is that 1.75%
may be suﬃcient before government support measures weigh on
headline inﬂation from next April. At the same time, government
pressure to avoid excessive tightening will ramp up as it tries to
keep the cost of capital low in what should be a big year for

Japanese investment.
People's Bank of China
The PBoC will remain in easing mode despite global tightening
pressures. China’s economic backdrop remains conducive to further
policy easing. First, inﬂation remains very mild, likely to end the
year under 1%, well below the 2% target set at the Two Sessions.
Second, growth has moderated and become more unbalanced,
raising risks for the economy. Domestic activity data weakened to
the worst levels since the pandemic, and could clearly beneﬁt from

further policy support. Third, the labour market looks soft, with
lower wage growth and high youth unemployment.
The PBoC has preferred targeted easing measures rather than
policy rate cuts. Bank net interest margins hit record lows and
global central banks shifted into rate hike mode in 2026. We still
see scope for both in 2027, especially if external demand starts to
show signs of weakening. We currently look for a 10bp rate cut in
mid-2027.

## Document 419: The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-11).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#fomc #usd #us
The FOMC pause to reflect
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
08:14 October 29 2026 Share
No change in October, though confidence in the economy's strength and two dissents point
to a hike in December.
The FOMC held at their October meeting, albeit with two dissents, Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed
President Hammack preferring to hike by 25bps at this meeting. The statement was short and, in the
press conference, Chair Warsh again declined to provide definitive guidance on the near-term policy

### Page 2

Related articles
outlook.
The Committee remains positive on economic activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding
at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Despite elevated uncertainty, “domestic
spending has been resilient”, underpinned by “strong” productivity growth and “robust” capital
investment. Chair Warsh subsequently also emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment. Labour demand and supply were represented as balanced and resilient in

both the statement and press conference.
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains
elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual
consumer inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
Along with the two dissents, this guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help the
Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter,

the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through 2027.
Towards the end of the press conference, Chair Warsh also announced a structural change in the
FOMC schedule. While there will still be 8 meetings in 2027, a meeting in the fall will focus on
monetary policy strategy versus the current stance of monetary policy. Chair Warsh then intends for 2
of the 8 meetings in 2028 to have this strategy focus. If realised, this will see the FOMC review and

vote on the current stance of monetary policy just 6 times per calendar year from 2028.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
3
OVERVIEW
Coming into the home strait for 2026,
the Australian consumer has again
got the staggers. Spending has held
up surprisingly well given the intense
headwinds that have buffeted household
budgets over the year. But the same cannot
be said for sentiment which has fallen off
badly in the face of more shocks.

Our October Red Book surveys the scene.
Psychologically, consumers has seen a
return to the ‘cost of living nightmare’
they struggled with through much of
2022, 2023 and 2024. Global forces
and domestic factors are again having
pervasive negative impacts on budgets.
The scale may not be as severe as the
previous episode – inflation is running in
the 3-4% range not the 7-8% and interest
rates have risen 1ppt not 4 – but the
feeling is similar. In part this because there

has was very little reprieve in between –
despite tax cuts and a partial unwind of the
interest rate tightening in 2025, household
incomes never got a chance to regain the
lost ground.
The inflation ‘relapse’, and the difficulty
the RBA and governments seem to be
having in managing the situation, is likely
feeding into fears about the outlook for the
wider economy as well.
Exactly what this means for spending is
not entirely clear. As discussed in our last

report , sentiment reads have been much
weaker than actual spending in recent
years. The apparent disconnect is likely
due to a mix of legacy effects from COVID,
and the specific challenges posed by high
inflation (including the lingering effects
of high prices even once inflation has
subsided).
On balance we expect the spending side
to slow materially, particularly as we
head into year end. Indeed, the seven
weeks between now and Christmas
could prove decisive for the consumer

outlook, beginning with another important
sentiment survey update and then the high
season for retail. How spending performs
outside of key sales windows will be most
telling.
Staggering into year end

1. Consumer spending: slowdown ahead
“... sentiment ... has fallen off badly in the face of

### Page 4

or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
4
The Westpac–Melbourne Institute
Consumer Sentiment Index declined 4.2%
over the three months to Oct, unwinding a
mini-rally through the middle of the year.
At 80.4 the average read over the Oct
survey implies that pessimists outnumber
optimists by nearly 20%, a deeply
pessimistic level by historical standards

that is amongst the forty worst reads since
the survey began in the 1973.
The survey detail shows concerns are still
primarily about the cost-of-living and high
interest rates than the more acute job and
insolvency problems that arise during a
recession. The RBA’s Sep rate hike, mid-
way through the survey week looks to have
badly rattled consumers.
The component detail shows a broad
pull-back but with bigger declines in
assessments of family finances and

buyer sentiment and milder declines in
assessments of the economic outlook.
Consumers remain intensely risk averse.
The Westpac Risk Aversion Index held near
historical highs in Sep, readings still only a
touch of the 52-year high of 60.8 recorded
in June 2023. Recall that the index is based
on responses to questions on the ‘wisest
place for savings’. Responses show ‘safe
options’ remain very heavily favoured.
The sentiment mix points to a significant

weakening in spending momentum is
weakening. CSI±, a modified indicator
that shows a better correlation with per
capita spending, points to a shift back to
significant outright contractions in per
capita spending. That said, spending
in 2026 has held up better than these
indicators have suggested so far.
Consumers are on high alert for interest
rate. The Westpac–Melbourne Institute
Mortgage Rate Expectations Index rose
5.5% to 179 over the three months to

Oct, just shy of May’s cycle peak of 181.
The RBA has raised interest rates 25bps
since then. The Nov survey update due Nov
10 will be an important update on how
expectations have evolved.
The ‘time to buy a major item’ index
dropped 4.3% over the last three months,
taking it back near the lows seen earlier
in the year and a full 40pts below the
long run avg. Durables spend has been
mixed over the first half of 2026, posting a
surprisingly solid gain in Q2 but with most

of this centred on new vehicle spend where
‘extenuating circumstances’ were at play.
Homebuyer sentiment has been
understandably unsettled in recent months
given heightened uncertainty around
interest rate and tax policy changes amid
evidence of a broadening downturn in
established markets.
The ‘time to buy a dwelling’ index actually
lifted 3.6% over the three months to Oct
but is still at firmly pessimistic levels. Both
monthly reads and sub-group detail have

been very unsettled.
The broadening and deepening downturn
in Australia’s housing markets is seeing
an equally rapid shift in consumer house
price expectations although these have not
turned as negative as they did during the
2022-23 correction.
The Westpac–Melbourne Institute
Consumer House Price Expectations Index
declined a further 2.4% over the 3mths to
Oct, following a steep 25% drop over the
previous 3mths. At 115.6, the Oct index
read is miles below the 15yr high of 174 at

the start of the year and below the long run
avg of 130. That said, the read is still above
100, meaning more consumers expect
prices to rise than fall.
The labour market remains an island of
relative calm for consumers. The Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Unemployment
Expectations Index rose 9.3% over the
three months to Oct, reversing most of
the 12.1% improvement over the previous
three months (recall that higher reads mean
more consumers expect unemployment to

rise over the year ahead). At 142.2, the Oct
index read is now decisively above the long
run avg of 129 again but  still well below
the levels that would sound more worrying
alarms.
As noted, the Nov survey, currently in the
field and due to be released on Nov 10,
will provide critical updates on many of
these themes.
Quick run-down

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
5
THE CONSUMER MOOD
• Consumers remain stuck in a cost-of-
living nightmare that seems to have
not end in sight. Sentiment remains
deeply pessimistic, weighed down by
continued uncertainty about the path
for inflation and interest rates, the
latter providing a clear negative ‘jolt’
midway through the Oct survey week.

• The Westpac Melbourne Institute
Index of Consumer Sentiment declined
4.2% over the three months to Oct,
unwinding a mini-rally through the
middle of the year. At 80.4 the average
read over the Oct survey implies that
pessimists outnumber optimists by
nearly 20%, a deeply pessimistic level
by historical standards. It is amongst
the forty worst reads since the survey
began in the 1973. We have had
two others in this unfortunate group
already this year (in Apr and Jun) with

another eight in 2022–23. Altogether
this marks the worst period of recurring
extremely weak sentiment since the
disastrous recession in the early 1990s.
• The survey detail shows consumer
concerns are still primarily about the
cost-of-living and high interest rates
than the more acute job and insolvency
problems that arise during a recession.
• Average weekly pump prices pushed
back over $2.30/litre nationally in early
Oct, rising to over $2.35/litre since. This

is near the peaks seen back in April and
up nearly 25% since the start of the
year. Meanwhile the RBA interest rate
rise in late Sep took the cash rate to
4.6%, the highest since 2011, with the
standard variable mortgage interest
rate pushing above 9% for the first time
since 2008.
• The RBA’s Sep move badly rattled
consumers. Sentiment amongst the
60% of the sample surveyed prior came
in at 86.9, up slightly from the Sep
read. The sentiment read across the

40% surveyed after the RBA decision
came in at just 67.2, an alarmingly weak
read that, for complete surveys, has
only been registered during the depths
of the early 1990s recession. The nearly
20% drop between pre and post RBA
samples is also the largest negative
swing we have seen since we started
tracking daily responses in 2019.
• The RBA has delivered another hike as
we go to press, meaning Nov is likely
to see yet another interest rate hike

to sentiment (the survey result will be
released on Nov 10).

3. Consumer sentiment: finances, economic conditions

### Page 6

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
6
THE CONSUMER MOOD
• The component detail shows a broad
pull-back but with bigger declines in
assessments of family finances and
buyer sentiment. The ‘finances vs a
year ago’ sub-index dropped 6% over
the 3mths to Oct  to just 66.9, on a par
with April’s extreme low. The pressure
is not expected to ease up any time

soon, the ‘finances, next 12 months’
sub-index also down 8.3% to 88.4, in
the bottom 10% of reads for this sub-
index since the survey began.
• Consumer views on the economy
recorded milder declines, the ‘economy,
next 12 months’ sub-index unchanged
on Jul, holding at 78.3 and the
‘economy, next 5 years’ sub-index down
1.8% to 85.5. However, the latter is now
back near the lows seen in Aug 2020.
This was a dark time during the COVID
crisis when Melbourne had entered

its second lockdown and just prior to
the Federal government significantly
expanding its JobKeeper supports. The
readings suggest there are shades of
COVID now with a similar sense of
helplessness – that Australia is unable
to avoid a global inflation problem and
that there is relatively little authorities
can do to help the situation that does
not add to pressures on consumers.
• The cost of living and interest rate
pressures are both broad and intense.

Pessimists outnumber optimists in 102
of the 106 sub-groups we track, and by
over 20% in nearly two thirds of these.
This speaks to the pervasive influence
of higher fuel costs in particular.
• That said, sentiment is becoming more
dispersed. It is possible to compute
sentiment index scores for each
individual respondent. Chart 4 shows
how the spread across these scores
has evolved since 1995. The average
standard deviation over the full history
is 46pts. It was about 4pts lower over

the decade prior to COVID but has risen
steadily since to over 52pts in Oct, the
average spread over the last 12mths
marking a record high. Chart 5 shows
the full distribution of individual scores
in the last 3mths compared to history.
• We noted in our last report that the
widening spread has been accompanied
by a notable divergence in sentiment
across major and minor party voters.
There are likely multiple underlying
reasons for the shift which the voter

group split may be picking up indirectly
rather than directly due to the way these
skew across different demographics.
